572 comparisons among the 293 accepted declarations. [Source hashes and review provenance](CoeffNorm--Taylor.json).

| `MvPolynomial.taylor` | `Roth.normOne` | no | A: Defines multivariate polynomial translation by a vector as a bundled coefficient-algebra endomorphism. Mechanism: The universal evaluation homomorphism aeval substitutes X j+C(α j) for each variable, automatically preserving sums, products, and coefficients. B: Defines the sum of absolute values of the integer coefficients, valued in the real numbers. Mechanism: Sums coefficient magnitudes over the finite monomial support. Taylor gives exact translation/substitution identities and finite expansion. CoeffNorm gives quantitative coefficient/evaluation bounds; triangle inequalities lose cancellation, so exact Taylor reconstruction is not equivalent to those inequalities. Diagonal substitution sends every variable to one common indeterminate rather than translating independent generators. Universal substitution primitives can be shared, while the degree/support and coefficient-merging estimates remain separate substantive consequences. | Retain the distinct conclusions; coordinate common monomial/finite-support/substitution primitives through their already reviewed APIs. No new pair-specific parametric theorem is justified by these different outputs. |
| `MvPolynomial.taylor` | `Roth.diag` | no | Both are aeval specializations. Translation remains an endomorphism on multivariate polynomials and sends Xj to Xj+Cαj. Diagonalization changes codomain to univariate polynomials and sends all variables to X, potentially merging exponents. | Both local semantic aliases may remain useful; generic aeval supplies shared construction, with distinct targets. |
